+AOcciqIgPj4e+/btw9SpU83xqkpYteU9HHm7Bf62rnC2RjikisTFSMPV9m4ceucannvVDn7p0qVY
+uHAhgsEggsEgQqFQlAlZnVm0PjOxIKFpGogIqqrC5XKhuroa06dPt4wDcl78F+qPNeNKe6cRoTjs

+        </td>
+        <td class="content">
+         If you fail to remove replication jobs from a node before
+removing the node itself, the replication job will become irremovable.
+Note that replication automatically switches direction when a
+replicated VM is migrated. Therefore, migrating a replicated VM from a
+node that is going to be deleted will set up replication jobs to that
+node automatically.
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+      </table>
+     </div>
+     <div class="ulist">
+      <ul>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Ensure that the remaining Ceph cluster has sufficient storage space
+  and that the OSDs are running (i.e.
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          up
+         </span>
+         and
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          in
+         </span>
+         ). The destruction
+  of any OSD, especially the last one on a node, will trigger a data
+  rebalance in Ceph.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+      </ul>
+     </div>
+     <div class="admonitionblock">
+      <table>
+       <tr>

+        </td>
+        <td class="content">
+         By default, Ceph pools have a
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          size/min_size
+         </span>
+         of
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          3/2
+         </span>
+         and a
+full node as
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          failure domain
+         </span>
+         at the object balancer
+         <a href="#pve_ceph_device_classes">
+          CRUSH
+         </a>
+         . So if less than
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          size
+         </span>
+         (
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          3
+         </span>
+         )
+nodes with running OSDs are online, data redundancy will be degraded.
+If less than
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          min_size
+         </span>
+         are online, pool I/O will be blocked and
+affected guests may crash.
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+      </table>
+     </div>
+     <div class="ulist">
+      <ul>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Ensure that sufficient
+         <a href="#pve_ceph_monitors">
+          monitors
+         </a>
+         ,
+         <a href="#pve_ceph_manager">
+          managers
+         </a>
+         and, if using CephFS,
+         <a href="#pveceph_fs_mds">
+          metadata servers
+         </a>
+         remain available in the Ceph
+  cluster.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+      </ul>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+    <div class="sect3">
+     <h4 id="_remove_the_cluster_node">
+      5.5.2. Remove the Cluster Node
+      <a class="headerlink" href="#_remove_the_cluster_node" title="Permalink to this heading">
+      </a>
+     </h4>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       Before a node can be removed from a cluster, you must ensure that it
+is no longer part of a Ceph cluster and that no Ceph resources or
+services are residing on it.
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       In the following example, the cluster node
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        node4
+       </span>
+       will be removed from both
+the Ceph-, and Proxmox VE corosync-cluster.
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="listingblock">
+      <div class="content monospaced">
+       <pre>node4# pvecm nodes
+
+Membership information
+~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
+    Nodeid      Votes Name
+         1          1 node1
+         2          1 node2
+         3          1 node3
+         4          1 node4 (local)</pre>
+      </div>
+     </div>
+     <div class="olist arabic">
+      <ol class="arabic">
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Start by removing the node from the Ceph cluster, this consists of multiple
+  steps:
+        </p>
+        <div class="olist loweralpha">
+         <ol class="loweralpha">
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            Start by
+            <a href="#pve_ceph_osd_destroy">
+             destroying
+            </a>
+            the remaining Ceph OSDs on the
+   node to be deleted, one after another.
+           </p>
+          </li>
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            Wait until the
+            <a href="#pve_ceph_mon_and_ts">
+             CEPH status
+            </a>
+            reaches
+            <span class="monospaced">
+             HEALTH_OK
+            </span>
+            again.
+           </p>
+          </li>
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            If any exists, destroy the remaining
+            <a href="#pveceph_fs_mds">
+             Ceph metadata servers
+            </a>
+            via the web interface at
+            <em>
+             Ceph → CephFS
+            </em>
+            or by running:
+           </p>
+           <div class="listingblock">
+            <div class="content monospaced">
+             <pre>node4# pveceph mds destroy node4</pre>
+            </div>
+           </div>
+          </li>
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            <a href="#pveceph_destroy_mon">
+             Destroy the remaining Ceph monitor.
+            </a>
+           </p>
+          </li>
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            <a href="#pveceph_destroy_mgr">
+             Destroy the remaining Ceph manager.
+            </a>
+           </p>
+          </li>
+          <li>
+           <p>
+            Finally, remove the now empty Ceph bucket (Proxmox VE node to be removed) from the
+   CRUSH hierarchy.
+           </p>
+           <div class="listingblock">
+            <div class="content monospaced">
+             <pre>node4# ceph osd crush remove node4</pre>
+            </div>
+           </div>
+          </li>
+         </ol>
+        </div>
+       </li>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Now, power off
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          node4
+         </span>
+         and ensure that it will not power on again in this
+  network with its current configuration.
+        </p>
+        <div class="admonitionblock">
+         <table>
+          <tr>
+           <td class="icon">
+            <img alt="Important" src="data:image/png;base64,

+          <pre>node1# pvecm nodes
+

+~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

+    <div class="sect3">
+     <h4 id="_cleanup_steps_after_node_removal">
+      5.5.3. Cleanup Steps After Node Removal
+      <a class="headerlink" href="#_cleanup_steps_after_node_removal" title="Permalink to this heading">
+      </a>
+     </h4>
+     <div class="ulist">
+      <ul>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         The configuration files of the removed node will still reside in
+         <em>
+          /etc/pve/nodes/node4
+         </em>
+         in the cluster filesystem. Recover any
+  configuration you still need and remove the directory afterwards.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         The SSH fingerprint of the removed node will still reside in the
+         <em>
+          known_hosts
+         </em>
+         file on the other nodes. If you receive an SSH error
+  after rejoining a node with the same IP or hostname, run
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          pvecm
+  updatecerts
+         </span>
+         once on the re-added node to update its fingerprint
+  cluster wide.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Existing
+         <a href="#ha_manager_rules">
+          high availability rules
+         </a>
+         will still
+  reference the deleted node, therefore remove the node from any
+  existing rules.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         The public key of the removed node will still be listed in
+         <em>
+          /etc/pve/priv/authorized_keys
+         </em>
+         and should be deleted if it is no
+  longer needed.
+        </p>
+       </li>
+      </ul>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <div class="title">
+       Rejoin the same node again:
+      </div>
+      <p>
+       If you want the same server to join the same cluster again, you have to:
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="ulist">
+      <ul>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         Reinstall Proxmox VE on the server,
+        </p>
+       </li>
+       <li>
+        <p>
+         then
+         <a href="#pvecm_join_node_to_cluster">
+          rejoin the node to the cluster
+         </a>
+        </p>
+       </li>
+      </ul>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+   </div>
+   <div class="sect2">
+    <h3 id="pvecm_separate_node_without_reinstall">
+     <span>
+      5.6. Separate a Node Without Reinstalling
+     </span>
+     <a class="headerlink" href="#pvecm_separate_node_without_reinstall" title="Permalink to this heading">
+     </a>
+    </h3>
     <div class="admonitionblock">
      <table>
       <tr>

        </td>
        <td class="content">
-        Failure to remove replication jobs to a node before removing said node
-will result in the replication job becoming irremovable. Especially note that
-replication automatically switches direction if a replicated VM is migrated, so
-by migrating a replicated VM from a node to be deleted, replication jobs will be
-set up to that node automatically.
+        This is
+        <strong>
+         not
+        </strong>
+        the recommended method, proceed with caution. Use the
+previous method if you’re unsure.
        </td>
       </tr>
      </table>
     </div>
     <div class="paragraph">
      <p>
-      If the node to be removed has been configured for
-      <a href="#chapter_pveceph">
-       Ceph
-      </a>
-      :
+      You can also separate a node from a cluster without reinstalling it from
+scratch. But after removing the node from the cluster, it will still have
+access to any shared storage. This must be resolved before you start removing
+the node from the cluster. A Proxmox VE cluster cannot share the exact same
+storage with another cluster, as storage locking doesn’t work over the cluster
+boundary. Furthermore, it may also lead to VMID conflicts.
      </p>

-           of
-           <span class="monospaced">
-            3/2
-           </span>
-           and a
-full node as
-           <span class="monospaced">
-            failure domain
-           </span>
-           at the object balancer
-           <a href="#pve_ceph_device_classes">
-            CRUSH
-           </a>
-           . So if less than
-           <span class="monospaced">
-            size
-           </span>
-           (
-           <span class="monospaced">
-            3
-           </span>
-           )
-nodes with running OSDs are online, data redundancy will be degraded.
-If less than
-           <span class="monospaced">
-            min_size
-           </span>
-           are online, pool I/O will be blocked and
-affected guests may crash.
-          </td>
-         </tr>
-        </table>
-       </div>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        Ensure that sufficient
-        <a href="#pve_ceph_monitors">
-         monitors
-        </a>
-        ,
-        <a href="#pve_ceph_manager">
-         managers
-        </a>
-        and, if using CephFS,
-        <a href="#pveceph_fs_mds">
-         metadata servers
-        </a>
-        remain available.
-       </p>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        To maintain data redundancy, each destruction of an OSD, especially
-the last one on a node, will trigger a data rebalance. Therefore,
-ensure that the OSDs on the remaining nodes have sufficient free space
-left.
-       </p>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        To remove Ceph from the node to be deleted, start by
-        <a href="#pve_ceph_osd_destroy">
-         destroying
-        </a>
-        its OSDs, one after the other.
-       </p>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        Once the
-        <a href="#pve_ceph_mon_and_ts">
-         CEPH status
-        </a>
-        is
-        <span class="monospaced">
-         HEALTH_OK
-        </span>
-        again,
-proceed by:
-       </p>
-       <div class="olist arabic">
-        <ol class="arabic">
-         <li>
-          <p>
-           destroying its
-           <a href="#pveceph_fs_mds">
-            metadata server
-           </a>
-           via web
-interface at
-           <em>
-            Ceph → CephFS
-           </em>
-           or by running:
-          </p>
-          <div class="listingblock">
-           <div class="content monospaced">
-            <pre># pveceph mds destroy &lt;local hostname&gt;</pre>
-           </div>
-          </div>
-         </li>
-         <li>
-          <p>
-           <a href="#pveceph_destroy_mon">
-            destroying its monitor
-           </a>
-          </p>
-         </li>
-         <li>
-          <p>
-           <a href="#pveceph_destroy_mgr">
-            destroying its manager
-           </a>
-          </p>
-         </li>
-        </ol>
-       </div>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        Finally, remove the now empty bucket (Proxmox VE node to be removed) from
-the CRUSH hierarchy by running:
-       </p>
-       <div class="listingblock">
-        <div class="content monospaced">
-         <pre># ceph osd crush remove &lt;hostname&gt;</pre>
-        </div>
-       </div>
-      </li>
-     </ol>
     </div>
     <div class="paragraph">
      <p>
-      In the following example, we will remove the node hp4 from the cluster.
+      It’s suggested that you create a new storage, where only the node which you want
+to separate has access. This can be a new export on your NFS or a new Ceph
+pool, to name a few examples. It’s just important that the exact same storage
+does not get accessed by multiple clusters. After setting up this storage, move
+all data and VMs from the node to it. Then you are ready to separate the
+node from the cluster.
      </p>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      Log in to a
-      <strong>
-       different
-      </strong>
-      cluster node (not hp4), and issue a
-      <span class="monospaced">
-       pvecm nodes
-      </span>
-      command to identify the node ID to remove:
-     </p>
-    </div>
-    <div class="listingblock">
-     <div class="content monospaced">
-      <pre> hp1# pvecm nodes
-
-Membership information
-~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
-    Nodeid      Votes Name
-         1          1 hp1 (local)
-         2          1 hp2
-         3          1 hp3
-         4          1 hp4</pre>
-     </div>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      At this point, you must power off hp4 and ensure that it will not power on
-again (in the network) with its current configuration.
-     </p>
-    </div>

-       </td>
-       <td class="content">
-        As mentioned above, it is critical to power off the node
-        <strong>
-         before
-        </strong>
-        removal, and make sure that it will
-        <strong>
-         not
-        </strong>
-        power on again
-(in the existing cluster network) with its current configuration.
-If you power on the node as it is, the cluster could end up broken,
-and it could be difficult to restore it to a functioning state.
-       </td>
-      </tr>
-     </table>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      After powering off the node hp4, we can safely remove it from the cluster.
-     </p>
-    </div>
-    <div class="listingblock">
-     <div class="content monospaced">
-      <pre> hp1# pvecm delnode hp4
- Killing node 4</pre>
-     </div>
     </div>
     <div class="admonitionblock">
      <table>
@@ -21301,246 +21682,21 @@
 PAr9L8j/96+U/w3yCoCXW14B8HLLvwDd67nwZIEPdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg=="/>
        </td>
        <td class="content">
-        At this point, it is possible that you will receive an error message
-stating
-        <span class="monospaced">
-         Could not kill node (error = CS_ERR_NOT_EXIST)
-        </span>
-        . This does not
-signify an actual failure in the deletion of the node, but rather a failure in
-corosync trying to kill an offline node. Thus, it can be safely ignored.
+        If your configuration contains a QDevice, you will need to remove it
+before removing the cluster node, as outlined in the FAQ
+        <a href="#_adding_deleting_nodes_after_qdevice_setup">
+         here
+        </a>
+        .
        </td>
       </tr>
      </table>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      Use
-      <span class="monospaced">
-       pvecm nodes
-      </span>
-      or
-      <span class="monospaced">
-       pvecm status
-      </span>
-      to check the node list again. It should
-look something like:
-     </p>
-    </div>
-    <div class="listingblock">
-     <div class="content monospaced">
-      <pre>hp1# pvecm status
-
-...
-
-Votequorum information
-~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
-Expected votes:   3
-Highest expected: 3
-Total votes:      3
-Quorum:           2
-Flags:            Quorate
-
-Membership information
-~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
-    Nodeid      Votes Name
-0x00000001          1 192.168.15.90 (local)
-0x00000002          1 192.168.15.91
-0x00000003          1 192.168.15.92</pre>
-     </div>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      If, for whatever reason, you want this server to join the same cluster again,
-you have to:
-     </p>
-    </div>
-    <div class="ulist">
-     <ul>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        do a fresh install of Proxmox VE on it,
-       </p>
-      </li>
-      <li>
-       <p>
-        then join it, as explained in the previous section.
-       </p>
-      </li>
-     </ul>
-    </div>
-    <div class="paragraph">
-     <p>
-      The configuration files for the removed node will still reside in
-      <em>
-       /etc/pve/nodes/hp4
-      </em>
-      . Recover any configuration you still need and remove the
-directory afterwards.
-     </p>

-     </div>
-     <div class="paragraph">
-      <p>
-       You can also separate a node from a cluster without reinstalling it from
-scratch. But after removing the node from the cluster, it will still have
-access to any shared storage. This must be resolved before you start removing
-the node from the cluster. A Proxmox VE cluster cannot share the exact same
-storage with another cluster, as storage locking doesn’t work over the cluster
-boundary. Furthermore, it may also lead to VMID conflicts.
-      </p>
-     </div>
-     <div class="paragraph">
-      <p>
-       It’s suggested that you create a new storage, where only the node which you want
-to separate has access. This can be a new export on your NFS or a new Ceph
-pool, to name a few examples. It’s just important that the exact same storage
-does not get accessed by multiple clusters. After setting up this storage, move
-all data and VMs from the node to it. Then you are ready to separate the
-node from the cluster.
-      </p>
-     </div>

+GqpKCIE8z5mdnUVVqVarbN++nba2Nvbu3csDDzyAqrJ8+atb+zjn6e/vZ/Xq1axatYq1a9fS29tL
+lmUMDQ1RKpXOm9EXvV6v1+tWr9eZnp5mZmaGWq1GjLHF62aiNZ+bnPfe45xrvTZzxMyYmZk56+LX
+zKjX661DvHOOJElIkoRSqYT3/vLvRlW15eHCa4VxzdvmpjZDfubzuXeb3vuzqCEixBhbo0NTkyS5
+PAr9L8j/96+U/w3yCoCXW14B8HLLvwDd67nwZIEPdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg=="/>
+        </td>
+        <td class="content">
+         A word of caution! It is critical that the change of the used networks by
+Ceph is being done carefully. Otherwise it could lead to a broken Ceph cluster
+with downtime to get it back into a working state! We recommend doing a trial
+run of the procedure in a (virtual) test cluster before changing the production
+infrastructure.
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+      </table>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       After the new network has been configured on all hosts, make sure you test it
+before proceeding with the changes. One way is to ping all hosts on the new
+network. If you use a large MTU, make sure to also test that it works. For
+example by sending ping packets that will result in a final packet at the max
+MTU size.
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       To test an MTU of 9000, you will need the following packet sizes:
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="admonitionblock">
+      <table>
+       <tr>

+        <td class="content">
+         We assume a simple network configuration. In more complicated setups, you
+might need to configure a lower MTU to account for any headers that might be
+added once a packet leaves the host.
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+      </table>
+     </div>
+     <div class="hdlist">
+      <table>
+       <tr>
+        <td class="hdlist1">
+         IPv4
+         <br/>
+        </td>
+        <td class="hdlist2">
+         <p style="margin-top: 0;">
+          The overhead of IP and ICMP is
+          <em>
+           28
+          </em>
+          bytes; the resulting packet size for
+the ping then is
+          <em>
+           8972
+          </em>
+          bytes.
+         </p>
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+       <tr>
+        <td class="hdlist1">
+         IPv6
+         <br/>
+        </td>
+        <td class="hdlist2">
+         <p style="margin-top: 0;">
+          The overhead is
+          <em>
+           48
+          </em>
+          bytes and the resulting packet size is
+          <em>
+           8952
+          </em>
+          bytes.
+         </p>
+        </td>
+       </tr>
+      </table>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       The resulting ping command will look like this for an IPv4:
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="listingblock">
+      <div class="content">
+       <!-- Generator: GNU source-highlight
+by Lorenzo Bettini
+http://www.lorenzobettini.it
+http://www.gnu.org/software/src-highlite -->
+       <pre><tt>ping -M <span style="font-weight: bold"><span style="color: #0000FF">do</span></span> -s <span style="color: #993399">8972</span> {target IP}</tt></pre>
+      </div>
+     </div>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       When you are switching between IPv4 and IPv6 networks, you need to make sure
+that the following options in the
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        ceph.conf
+       </span>
+       file are correctly set to
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        true
+       </span>
+       or
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        false
+       </span>
+       . These config options configure if Ceph services should bind to IPv4
+or IPv6 addresses.
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="listingblock">
+      <div class="content monospaced">
+       <pre>ms_bind_ipv4 = true
+ms_bind_ipv6 = false</pre>
+      </div>
+     </div>
+     <div class="sect4">
+      <h5 id="pveceph_network_change_public">
+       Change the Ceph Public Network
+       <a class="headerlink" href="#pveceph_network_change_public" title="Permalink to this heading">
+       </a>
+      </h5>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The Ceph Public network is the main communication channel in a Ceph cluster
+between the different services and clients (for example, a VM). Changing it to
+a different network is not as simple as changing the Ceph Cluster network. The
+main reason is that besides the configuration in the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ceph.conf
+        </span>
+        file, the Ceph
+MONs (monitors) have an internal configuration where they keep track of all the
+other MONs that are part of the cluster, the
+        <em>
+         monmap
+        </em>
+        .
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        Therefore, the procedure to change the Ceph Public network is a bit more
+involved:
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="olist arabic">
+       <ol class="arabic">
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Change
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           public_network
+          </span>
+          in the
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           /etc/pve/ceph.conf
+          </span>
+          file (do not change any
+other value)
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Restart non MON services: OSDs, MGRs and MDS on one host
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Wait until Ceph is back to
+          <em>
+           HEALTH_OK
+          </em>
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Verify services are using the new network
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Continue restarting services on the next host
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Destroy one MON
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Recreate MON
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Wait until Ceph is back to
+          <em>
+           HEALTH_OK
+          </em>
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Continue destroying and recreating MONs
+         </p>
+        </li>
+       </ol>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        You first need to edit the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         /etc/pve/ceph.conf
+        </span>
+        file. Change the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         public_network
+        </span>
+        line to match the new subnet.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="listingblock">
+       <div class="content monospaced">
+        <pre>public_network = 10.9.9.30/24</pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>
+      <div class="admonitionblock">
+       <table>
+        <tr>

+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        After you have changed the network, you need to restart the non MON services in
+the cluster for the changes to take effect. Do so one node at a time! To restart all
+non MON services on one node, you can use the following commands on that node.
+Ceph has
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         systemd
+        </span>
+        targets for each type of service.
+       </p>
+      </div>

+systemctl restart ceph-mgr<span style="color: #990000">.</span>target
+systemctl restart ceph-mds<span style="color: #990000">.</span>target</tt></pre>

+         <td class="content">
+          After the first OSD service got restarted, the GUI will complain that
+the OSD is not reachable anymore. This is not an issue; VMs can still reach
+them. The reason for the message is that the MGR service cannot reach the OSD
+anymore. The error will vanish after the MGR services get restarted.
+         </td>

+      </div>

+         </td>
+         <td class="content">
+          Do not restart OSDs on multiple hosts at the same time. Chances are
+that for some PGs (placement groups), 2 out of the (default) 3 replicas will
+be down. This will result in I/O being halted until the minimum required number
+(
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           min_size
+          </span>
+          ) of replicas is available again.
+         </td>
+        </tr>
+       </table>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        To verify that the services are listening on the new network, you can run the
+following command on each node:
+       </p>
+      </div>

+       <div class="content">

+http://www.lorenzobettini.it
+http://www.gnu.org/software/src-highlite -->
+        <pre><tt>ss -tulpn <span style="color: #990000">|</span> grep ceph</tt></pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>

+         </td>
+         <td class="content">
+          Since OSDs will also listen on the Ceph Cluster network, expect to see that
+network too in the output of
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           ss -tulpn
+          </span>
+          .
+         </td>
+        </tr>
+       </table>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        Once the Ceph cluster is back in a fully healthy state (
+        <em>
+         HEALTH_OK
+        </em>
+        ), and the
+services are listening on the new network, continue to restart the services on
+the host.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The last services that need to be moved to the new network are the Ceph MONs
+themselves. The easiest way is to destroy and recreate each monitor one by
+one. This way, any mention of it in the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ceph.conf
+        </span>
+        and the monitor internal
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         monmap
+        </span>
+        is handled automatically.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        Destroy the first MON and create it again. Wait a few moments before you
+continue on to the next MON in the cluster, and make sure the cluster reports
+        <em>
+         HEALTH_OK
+        </em>
+        before proceeding.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        Once all MONs are recreated, you can verify that any mention of MONs in the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ceph.conf
+        </span>
+        file references the new network. That means mainly the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         mon_host
+        </span>
+        line and the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         [mon.HOSTNAME]
+        </span>
+        sections.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        One final
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ss -tulpn | grep ceph
+        </span>
+        should show that the old network is not used
+by any Ceph service anymore.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+     </div>
+     <div class="sect4">
+      <h5 id="pveceph_network_change_cluster">
+       Change the Ceph Cluster Network
+       <a class="headerlink" href="#pveceph_network_change_cluster" title="Permalink to this heading">
+       </a>
+      </h5>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The Ceph Cluster network is used for the replication traffic between the OSDs.
+Therefore, it can be beneficial to place it on its own fast physical network.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The overall procedure is:
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="olist arabic">
+       <ol class="arabic">
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Change
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           cluster_network
+          </span>
+          in the
+          <span class="monospaced">
+           /etc/pve/ceph.conf
+          </span>
+          file
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Restart OSDs on one host
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Wait until Ceph is back to
+          <em>
+           HEALTH_OK
+          </em>
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Verify OSDs are using the new network
+         </p>
+        </li>
+        <li>
+         <p>
+          Continue restarting OSDs on the next host
+         </p>
+        </li>
+       </ol>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        You first need to edit the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         /etc/pve/ceph.conf
+        </span>
+        file. Change the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         cluster_network
+        </span>
+        line to match the new subnet.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="listingblock">
+       <div class="content monospaced">
+        <pre>cluster_network = 10.9.9.30/24</pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>
+      <div class="admonitionblock">
+       <table>
+        <tr>
+         <td class="icon">

+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        After you have changed the network, you need to restart the OSDs in the cluster
+for the changes to take effect. Do so one node at a time!
+To restart all OSDs on one node, you can use the following command on the CLI on
+that node:
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="listingblock">
+       <div class="content">
+        <!-- Generator: GNU source-highlight
+by Lorenzo Bettini
+http://www.lorenzobettini.it
+http://www.gnu.org/software/src-highlite -->
+        <pre><tt>systemctl restart ceph-osd<span style="color: #990000">.</span>target</tt></pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>

+          <span class="monospaced">

+          </span>

+check the
+        <strong>
+         OSD Details → Network
+        </strong>
+        tab in the
+        <strong>
+         Ceph → OSD
+        </strong>
+        panel or by running
+the following command on the host:
+       </p>

+     </div>
+     <div class="sect4">
+      <h5 id="qm_secure_boot_ca_expiration">
+       Secure Boot Certificate Expiration
+       <a class="headerlink" href="#qm_secure_boot_ca_expiration" title="Permalink to this heading">
+       </a>
+      </h5>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The original set of Microsoft certificates from 2011, which Windows and common
+Linux distributions use for secure boot, expires in June 2026. Microsoft created
+replacement certificates in 2023
+        <span class="footnote">
+         <br/>
+         [Microsoft support article about the issue
+         <a href="https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/topic/windows-secure-boot-certificate-expiration-and-ca-updates-7ff40d33-95dc-4c3c-8725-a9b95457578e">
+          https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/topic/windows-secure-boot-certificate-expiration-and-ca-updates-7ff40d33-95dc-4c3c-8725-a9b95457578e
+         </a>
+         ]
+         <br/>
+        </span>
+        .
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        The firmware only allows bootloaders signed with certificates present on the EFI
+disk. In particular, an EFI disk with only the 2011 certificates will reject
+bootloaders signed with the 2023 certificates. The
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ms-cert=2023k
+        </span>
+        marker in the
+VM configuration for an EFI disk indicates that the new certificates are
+enrolled.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        If the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         pve-edk2-firmware
+        </span>
+        package version is at least
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         4.2025.05-1
+        </span>
+        , newly
+created EFI disks contain both the 2011 and 2023 certificates and will have the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         ms-cert=2023k
+        </span>
+        marker. For EFI disks created before that, you need to enroll
+the certificates:
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        For Windows with BitLocker, run the following command inside PowerShell:
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="listingblock">
+       <div class="content monospaced">
+        <pre>manage-bde -protectors -disable &lt;drive&gt;</pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        For example,
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         &lt;drive&gt;
+        </span>
+        could be
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         C:
+        </span>
+        . This is required for each drive with
+BitLocker before proceeding. Otherwise, you will be prompted for the BitLocker
+recovery key on the next boot!
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        Select the EFI disk in the
+        <em>
+         Hardware
+        </em>
+        view in the UI and use
+        <em>
+         Disk Action &gt; Enroll Updated Certificates
+        </em>
+        . Alternatively, enroll the
+certificates via the
+        <span class="monospaced">
+         /nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/config
+        </span>
+        API endpoint. The
+enrollment takes effect when the VM is next started. The
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="listingblock">
+       <div class="content monospaced">
+        <pre>qm enroll-efi-keys &lt;vmid&gt;</pre>
+       </div>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        CLI command achieves the same, but requires the VM to be shut down.
+       </p>
+      </div>
+      <div class="paragraph">
+       <p>
+        For further steps on updating secure boot within Windows and signing the
+bootloader with the new 2023 certificates, refer to the Microsoft support
+articles about
+        <a href="https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/topic/enterprise-deployment-guidance-for-cve-2023-24932-88b8f034-20b7-4a45-80cb-c6049b0f9967">
+         enterprise deployment guidance for CVE-2023-24932
+        </a>
+        and the
+        <a href="https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2023-24932">
+         related security update
+        </a>
+        which needs to be installed first.
+       </p>
+      </div>

+    <div class="sect3">
+     <h4 id="ha_manager_fencing_status">
+      15.7.4. Fencing &amp; Watchdog Status
+      <a class="headerlink" href="#ha_manager_fencing_status" title="Permalink to this heading">
+      </a>
+     </h4>
+     <div class="paragraph">
+      <p>
+       The
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        ha-manager status
+       </span>
+       output includes a fencing entry that shows the CRM
+watchdog state. Each LRM entry additionally shows its own watchdog state.
+      </p>
+     </div>
+     <div class="dlist">
+      <dl>
+       <dt class="hdlist1">
+        armed
+       </dt>
+       <dd>
+        <p>
+         The CRM is actively managing services and has its watchdog open. Each node’s
+LRM also holds a watchdog while it has its agent lock. On quorum loss or
+daemon failure, the respective watchdog triggers a node reset to ensure safe
+failover.
+        </p>
+       </dd>
+       <dt class="hdlist1">
+        standby
+       </dt>
+       <dd>
+        <p>
+         The HA stack is ready but no CRM is actively running as master, for example
+when no HA resources are configured yet or the cluster just started. The CRM
+watchdog is not open. Fencing automatically transitions to
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          armed
+         </span>
+         once a CRM
+takes over as master.
+        </p>
+       </dd>
+       <dt class="hdlist1">
+        disarming
+       </dt>
+       <dd>
+        <p>
+         A
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          disarm-ha
+         </span>
+         command was issued. The CRM is freezing or suspending services
+from tracking and waiting for all LRMs to release their watchdogs. The CRM
+watchdog is still active during this phase. Each LRM entry’s watchdog status
+changes to
+         <span class="monospaced">
+          released
+         </span>
+         as it acknowledges the disarm.
+        </p>
+       </dd>
+       <dt class="hdlist1">
+        disarmed
+       </dt>
+       <dd>
+        <p>
+         All watchdogs have been released cluster-wide. No automatic fencing, failover,
+or recovery takes place.
+See
+         <a href="#ha_manager_disarm">
+          Disarming HA for Cluster Maintenance
+         </a>
+         .
+        </p>
+       </dd>
+      </dl>
+     </div>
+     <div class="admonitionblock">
+      <table>
+       <tr>

    <div class="sect2">
+    <h3 id="ha_manager_disarm">
+     <span>
+      15.12. Disarming HA for Cluster Maintenance
+     </span>
+     <a class="headerlink" href="#ha_manager_disarm" title="Permalink to this heading">
+     </a>
+    </h3>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      Certain cluster maintenance tasks, such as reconfiguring the network or the
+cluster communication stack (corosync), can cause temporary quorum loss or
+network partitions. Normally, HA would interpret this as a node failure and
+trigger self-fencing, disrupting services unnecessarily.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      The disarm mechanism releases all CRM and LRM watchdogs cluster-wide, allowing
+you to perform such maintenance safely without the risk of nodes being fenced.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="admonitionblock">
+     <table>
+      <tr>
+       <td class="icon">
+        <img alt="Important" src="data:image/png;base64,

+       </td>
+       <td class="content">
+        While disarmed, HA does not protect your services. Failures during
+this period are not automatically recovered. Keep the disarm window as short as
+possible.
+       </td>
+      </tr>
+     </table>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <div class="title">
+      Resource Modes
+     </div>
+     <p>
+      When disarming HA, you must choose a resource mode that controls how HA managed
+resources are handled while disarmed. The current state of resources is not
+affected.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="dlist">
+     <dl>
+      <dt class="hdlist1">
+       freeze
+      </dt>
+      <dd>
+       <p>
+        New commands and state changes are not applied. Services stay in their current
+state, but the HA stack does not react to failures or process new requests.
+This is the safest choice when you expect all nodes to remain running.
+       </p>
+      </dd>
+      <dt class="hdlist1">
+       ignore
+      </dt>
+      <dd>
+       <p>
+        Resources are suspended from HA tracking and can be managed as if they were not
+HA managed. This allows you to manually start, stop, or migrate services while
+HA is disarmed. Use this when you need to manually relocate services during
+maintenance. When re-arming, the CRM rechecks service locations against the
+configuration to pick up any manual migrations.
+       </p>
+      </dd>
+     </dl>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <div class="title">
+      Disarming and Re-Arming
+     </div>
+     <p>
+      To disarm HA with the desired resource mode:
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="listingblock">
+     <div class="content monospaced">
+      <pre># ha-manager crm-command disarm-ha freeze</pre>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      or:
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="listingblock">
+     <div class="content monospaced">
+      <pre># ha-manager crm-command disarm-ha ignore</pre>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      To re-arm HA after maintenance is complete:
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="listingblock">
+     <div class="content monospaced">
+      <pre># ha-manager crm-command arm-ha</pre>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      You can monitor the current state with:
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="listingblock">
+     <div class="content monospaced">
+      <pre># ha-manager status</pre>
+     </div>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      The fencing status line shows the current state of the fencing mechanism (see
+      <a href="#ha_manager_fencing_status">
+       Fencing Status
+      </a>
+      ), including the CRM and LRM
+watchdog states.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <div class="title">
+      The Disarm Process
+     </div>
+     <p>
+      After you request disarm, the following sequence happens:
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="olist arabic">
+     <ol class="arabic">
+      <li>
+       <p>
+        The CRM freezes all services or suspends them from tracking, depending on the
+  chosen resource mode.
+       </p>
+      </li>
+      <li>
+       <p>
+        Each LRM finishes its active workers, then releases its agent lock and
+  watchdog.
+       </p>
+      </li>
+      <li>
+       <p>
+        Once all online LRMs are idle, the CRM releases its own watchdog too.
+       </p>
+      </li>
+     </ol>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      The CRM keeps the manager lock throughout this process, so it can accept and
+process the
+      <span class="monospaced">
+       arm-ha
+      </span>
+      command to reverse it.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      If any services are currently being fenced, recovered, or migrated, the disarm
+is deferred until these operations complete. This ensures that services in the
+middle of a transition do not end up in an inconsistent state.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <div class="title">
+      Nodes Offline During Disarm
+     </div>
+     <p>
+      If a node is offline when HA is disarmed, its LRM cannot process the disarm
+request. The CRM proceeds to the disarmed state once all
+      <strong>
+       online
+      </strong>
+      LRMs have
+completed their part. The offline node does not block this.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      When the offline node comes back online while HA is still disarmed, its LRM
+picks up the disarm state and releases its watchdog without attempting any
+service recovery.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      When you re-arm HA, any services that were on the offline node are handled
+according to normal HA recovery rules: they are fenced and recovered if the node
+is still unreachable, or restarted on the node if it has come back online.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <div class="title">
+      Interaction with Maintenance Mode
+     </div>
+     <p>
+      If a node is already in maintenance mode when disarm is requested, the
+maintenance migration continues until all services have been moved away. Once no
+active services and workers remain, the LRM releases its lock and watchdog as
+part of the disarm process.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      When HA is re-armed, the maintenance mode state is preserved. The node remains
+in maintenance and services are not moved back until maintenance mode is
+explicitly disabled.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="admonitionblock">
+     <table>
+      <tr>

+        While the HA stack is disarmed, no automatic recovery, failover, or

     <div class="sect3">
      <h4 id="_basic_scheduler">

+      15.13.1. Basic Scheduler
       <a class="headerlink" href="#_basic_scheduler" title="Permalink to this heading">

+     <p>
+      <strong>
+       ha-manager crm-command disarm-ha
+      </strong>
+      <span class="monospaced">
+       &lt;resource-mode&gt;
+      </span>
+      <a class="headerlink" href="#cli_ha-manager_crm-command_disarm-ha" title="Permalink to this heading">
+      </a>
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="paragraph">
+     <p>
+      Request disarming the HA stack, releasing all watchdogs cluster-wide.
+     </p>
+    </div>
+    <div class="dlist">
+     <dl>
+      <dt class="hdlist1">
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        &lt;resource-mode&gt;
+       </span>
+       :
+       <span class="monospaced">
+        &lt;freeze | ignore&gt;
+       </span>
+      </dt>
+      <dd>
+       <p>
+        Controls how HA managed resources are handled while disarmed. The current state of resources is not affected.
+        <em>
+         freeze
+        </em>
+        : new commands and state changes are not applied.
+        <em>
+         ignore
+        </em>
+        : resources are removed from HA tracking and can be managed as if they were not HA managed.
+       </p>
+      </dd>
+     </dl>
